(i) CREATE-IDEMPOTENCY CHECK first (check BOTH durable markers — a prior self-registration may be recorded in either, since D3's own TRIGGER SELF-REGISTRATION step writes the same `entry_type`): `SELECT trigger_id FROM ops.catchup_refire_log WHERE routine = <routine> AND outcome = 'trigger_created' ORDER BY attempted_ts DESC LIMIT 1` and `SELECT * FROM events.decision_log WHERE entry_type = 'trigger-self-registration' AND <entry references this routine id> ORDER BY logged_ts DESC LIMIT 1` — if either returns a row, a trigger was already created for this routine and the repo file is merely stale/unmerged: REUSE that `trig_...` id (repair `ops/trigger_ids.json` with it, provenance `'api'`, commit + push), do NOT create a second trigger. (ii) Otherwise verify the routine has entries in BOTH `ops/cadence.yaml` and `ops/triggers.json` (the CI-validated surfaces) AND is not in the never-refire exclusion list above; if either check fails, treat as creation-failed. (iii) Otherwise: `RemoteTrigger create` (body mirrored from `RemoteTrigger get` on any known trigger id — mirrored bodies inherit `allowed_tools` including RemoteTrigger; instruction = `ops/triggers.json`'s instruction string for the routine VERBATIM; schedule = the routine's documented slot in `ops/cadence.yaml`'s WEB-UI TRIGGER AUDIT block, else an off-peak slot ≥30 min clear of every trigger sharing a possible calendar day — and remember API-created crons are fixed-UTC, NOT DST-aware, per the OPS0-own 04:30 UTC / 10:30 PM MDT convention). `CALL ops.sp_log_decision(...)` (`entry_type='trigger-self-registration'`, body naming routine + returned `trig_...` id + schedule) BEFORE any git operation — the durable marker step (i) reads on a future run. Record the returned `trig_` id in `ops/trigger_ids.json` (provenance `'api'`), commit + push. `INSERT INTO ops.catchup_refire_log (miss_key, routine, tier, trigger_id, outcome, note) VALUES (<miss_key>, <routine>, <tier>, <new id>, 'trigger_created', 'OPS0 self-registered missing trigger')`, then `CALL RemoteTrigger run(<new id>)` for the missed run and `CALL ops.sp_raise_alert('info','OPS0','catchup_trigger_created','<routine> had no live trigger — OPS0 created + re-fired it','<JSON: routine, trigger_id, schedule>')`. ONLY on creation failure / failed surface check: `INSERT ... outcome 'no_trigger_id' ...` and `CALL ops.sp_raise_alert('warning','OPS0','catchup_refire_no_trigger_id','<routine> missed <as_of> and trigger self-registration failed: <error>','<JSON>')`. (`ops/triggers.json`'s OPS0 entry is a one-line pointer with no duplicated step text — nothing to mirror there.) Note: the `'trigger_created'` row doubles as the miss_key idempotency marker (readiness excludes on ANY row per bigquery/59:90), so no additional `'refired'` row is required for that miss.
4. Either branch: the `ops.catchup_refire_log` INSERT is the idempotency marker — `state.catchup_refire_readiness` excludes this `miss_key` on every subsequent read today, so OPS0 never double-fires the same miss.

STEP 3 — WEEKLY TRIGGER-CONFIG SWEEP (trigger-config-drift audit, 2026-07-16, ARCH-2/CC-5 merge; Sundays only: skip unless `CURRENT_DATE("America/Denver")` is a Sunday — log a one-line "OPS0: not Sunday, trigger-config sweep skipped" heartbeat on a skip day, no Daily.md output). Run the identical `RemoteTrigger get` diff + self-correct procedure Q4 step E runs (source `ops/trigger_ids.json` + each routine's `expected_trigger` in `ops/cadence.yaml` + `ops/triggers.json`'s canonical instructions), verbatim: INSTRUCTION drift self-corrects on every cohort (`RemoteTrigger update` instruction-only + re-verify round-trip); SCHEDULE drift self-corrects ONLY on `recurrence: custom_cron` routines via the same snapshot + partial-update + verify + rollback-on-failure guard (and bootstraps any remaining `cron_utc: TO_POPULATE`); SCHEDULE drift on a `daily`/`weekly` routine and any missing-live-trigger / failed-round-trip case raise the same emailed `warning` `trigger_drift` / `trigger_missing_live` alert (the `daily`/`weekly` cohort is EMPTY as of 2026-08-01 — the whole fleet is `custom_cron`, so schedule drift now self-corrects everywhere; see Q4 step E and `ops/cadence.yaml`'s header for why the old DST-aware-GUI premise was false) with the paste-ready canonical schedule + instruction; a successful correction raises the same info `trigger_drift_corrected` alert. Never touch `enabled` status; never auto-create a trigger (that remains D3's TRIGGER SELF-REGISTRATION step / OPS0's own STEP 2 item 3 self-registration path, both scoped to catchup-missing triggers only — this sweep only diffs/corrects triggers that already exist). NEVER call `RemoteTrigger run` from this step. Also, for every routine in `ops/trigger_ids.json`, `RemoteTrigger get` the trigger and verify `job_config.ccr.session_context.allowed_tools` includes `"RemoteTrigger"` (root cause of the 2026-07-18 blocked-refire incident; config fix is owner-gated per the 2026-07-19 session — interactive RemoteTrigger update calls are classifier-blocked, so this sweep step self-heals it whenever a session with the tool and permission runs); if absent, `RemoteTrigger update` sending back the full `job_config` from the get with `allowed_tools` amended to append `"RemoteTrigger"` and nothing else changed, then `CALL ops.sp_raise_alert('info','OPS0','trigger_drift_corrected','<routine> trigger allowed_tools was missing RemoteTrigger — re-added','<JSON: routine, trigger_id>')` — config update only, STEP 3's existing never-call-RemoteTrigger-run rule still holds. **On total `RemoteTrigger get` failure this session (a tool outage, not per-id staleness) — mirrored from Q4 step E's FALLBACK 1/FALLBACK 2 ladder verbatim, per that section's own closing sentence, since OPS0 has no Chrome/console fallback of its own (it is always headless, so a total failure here goes straight to the FALLBACK-2 behavior):** `CALL ops.sp_raise_alert('warning','OPS0','trigger_audit_needs_chrome', <same message text, OPS0-sourced>, '<JSON: ..., drift_classes_uncovered:["schedule","allowed_tools"]>')` — the identical corrected, narrowed scope Q4 step E's FALLBACK 2 carries (2026-08-10): `instruction` and `enabled` drift both already have independent headless coverage elsewhere (`state.instruction_drift` nightly via `ops.sp_sq_cadence_check`; `state.trigger_attestation`/`state.cadence_watch` lagged) — see Q4 step E FALLBACK 2 for the full rationale, not restated here. Write ONE `events.decision_log` entry (`entry_type='trigger-audit'`, source `OPS0`) per sweep with checked/corrected/warned counts, alongside the existing per-miss STEP 2 logging.

STEP 4 — GIT LANDING SWEEP (landing-hardening 2026-07-29; daily, every run). A routine's own commit+push is unconditional and gate-free, but LANDING on `main` is not: auto-merge is fail-closed on CI (a branch whose exact tip SHA has no successful `ci.yml` run is skipped on every drain, with exactly ONE automatic content-free `gh run rerun --failed` per tip SHA — after that it is skipped indefinitely until the SHA changes or CI goes green), and a genuine merge conflict parks the branch behind an open `Auto-merge conflict: <branch>` PR that the CI-side workflow deliberately never resolves (`ops/RUNBOOK.md` §20: "automating a rebase on ambiguous conflict content was judged too risky to do blindly"). `.github/workflows/stranded-branch-check.yml` already detects both every 6h, but delivers ONLY to a GitHub issue — a surface no human monitors and no routine reads — so an unlanded day of routine output can sit indefinitely with nobody told. **This step puts that signal on the monitored channel (`ops.alerts` → `alert_emailer.gs` email) AND, where (d)'s six preconditions are all satisfiable, lands the stranded work itself.** **NON-GATING, best-effort, exactly like STEP 3 and the shared NON-GATING + FALLBACK rule: this step must NEVER halt OPS0 and never change STEP 1/2/3's outcomes. On ANY error — `git fetch` fails, `gh` absent or unauthenticated, a BigQuery read/UPDATE/INSERT in (c)/(e)/(f) errors — abort THIS step, note it in the run `<note>`, and continue. The non-gating rule covers this step's SQL calls exactly as it covers its git calls.**

(a) DETECT — pure git, no `gh` required. **HISTORY-DEPTH PRECHECK — run this FIRST, before any ancestry test in this step and before (f)'s history-window test (shallow-clone hardening 2026-08-04, closing `ops0_landing_sweep_defect`).** The routine container clones SHALLOW by default, and on a shallow clone the git checks below do not error — they return CONFIDENTLY WRONG answers that are indistinguishable from a real strand: MEASURED 2026-08-03, the clone was truncated at boundary `f5bbade` with 65 commits reachable from `origin/main` (972 after `--unshallow`), and in that state `git merge-base --is-ancestor` returned FALSE for `dcf92b5` / `9c52cfab` / `7beef36` — all three genuinely ON `main` — while `git log origin/main --since=...` returned nothing for windows that did contain commits. Run these three numbered steps in order, exactly as written — do NOT collapse them into a single "try to deepen, bail on any error" test, because the two failure modes below are opposite and must not be conflated: **1. ASK.** Run `git rev-parse --is-shallow-repository`. If it prints `false`, the clone is already complete: the history is trustworthy, **skip straight to DEEPENED-OK below and run STEP 4 normally — do NOT run the `--unshallow` fetch at all.** **2. DEEPEN (only when step 1 printed `true`).** Run `git fetch --unshallow origin`. **3. RE-ASK.** Run `git rev-parse --is-shallow-repository` again; `false` means the deepen worked. Treat the clone as UNDEEPENABLE only when step 3 still prints `true`, or when step 2 failed for a reason that genuinely left the history truncated (network failure, GitHub rate-limiting the deepen). **A `fatal: --unshallow on a complete repository does not make sense` error is NOT evidence of an undeepenable clone — it is proof of the OPPOSITE** (the repo is already complete; that error only appears if step 1 was misread and step 2 was run on a full clone, so treat it as DEEPENED-OK and proceed normally). This distinction matters because the skip path below is deliberately SILENT — misclassifying a healthy full clone as undeepenable would disable the entire landing sweep indefinitely with nothing to alert on, which is a worse failure than the one this precheck fixes. **DEEPENED-OK** — the tests below carry evidentiary weight; proceed. **UNDEEPENABLE** — treat every non-ancestor verdict below and every "no commit found" in (f) as **INCONCLUSIVE, never as evidence of a strand**: this run's DETECT yields ZERO candidate stranded branches (so (c) and (d) have nothing to act on), (e)'s ancestry test is skipped so no `stranded_branch` alert is resolved or re-raised on truncated evidence, (f) is skipped outright, and the run `<note>` must say `git history could not be deepened this run — (c)/(d)/(e)/(f) skipped, no strand/unlanded conclusions drawn`. This precheck exists because (a)'s verdict is what makes a branch eligible for (d)'s AUTONOMOUS ADOPT path: on a shallow clone an ALREADY-MERGED (or deliberately reverted) `claude/*` branch reads as stranded, and (d) would merge it back onto OPS0's own branch with no human in the loop. It did not bite on 2026-08-03 only because `origin` happened to carry zero `claude/*` refs. Prior record: `events.decision_log` entry `0e9a757e` (OPS0 2026-07-29) recommended exactly this fix after two false positives (D1 2026-07-22, D1 2026-07-25); routing it to a self-improvement audit did not land it, and OPS0 cannot self-correct it because plan prose is outside OPS0's `writes:` list. THEN: `git fetch origin '+refs/heads/*:refs/remotes/origin/*' --prune`, then for every `refs/remotes/origin/claude/*` ref that is NOT an ancestor of `origin/main` (`git merge-base --is-ancestor <ref> origin/main`), take the tip committer age; a healthy branch drains within ~30s of green CI, so treat age > 6h as stranded (same threshold as the CI-side check). EXCLUDE this session's own branch. (Deliberately scoped to `claude/*`, the routine fleet's own branches — narrower than `stranded-branch-check.yml`, which sweeps every prefix; a stranded `jack/*` branch is the operator's own work and not this routine's business.) **Outside (d)'s tightly-gated ADOPT path and the HISTORY-DEPTH PRECHECK's `--unshallow` fetch above, this step performs NO other git operation: it never checks out, rebases, cherry-picks, or resets, and it must leave the worktree exactly as it found it.**

(b) DIAGNOSE — best-effort enrichment only. If the `gh` CLI is available and authenticated (**NOT confirmed present in the routine container** — every other `gh` use in this repo lives in `.github/workflows/*.yml`; test it once, and on any failure record cause `unknown` and continue to (c), never halt): CI conclusion for the exact tip SHA (`gh api "repos/$GITHUB_REPOSITORY/actions/workflows/ci.yml/runs?head_sha=<sha>&per_page=1" --jq '.workflow_runs[0].conclusion'`) and whether an open PR titled exactly `Auto-merge conflict: <branch>` exists whose head SHA equals that same tip SHA. A missing diagnosis degrades the alert's detail, never its delivery.

(c) ALERT — for every stranded branch: `CALL ops.sp_raise_alert_once('warning','OPS0','stranded_branch','<branch> unmerged','<JSON: branch, tip_sha, age_h, cause, ci_conclusion, pr_url>')`. Use the `_once` dedup variant and keep BOTH the age AND the diagnosed cause OUT of the message — payload only. `sp_raise_alert_once` dedups on the exact `(category, message)` text (`bigquery/10_observability.sql`), so ANY field that can vary between runs defeats the dedup and lets undeduped rows accumulate — the documented failure mode behind `bigquery/111`'s comments. `<cause>` is exactly such a field: it flips between `unknown` and a real diagnosis as `gh` availability varies run to run, so embedding it would produce a fresh alert on every flip. Keep the message a stable `<branch> unmerged` so a branch stranded for a week produces one alert, not seven — matching the `owner_confirmation_stale` / `book_drawdown_soft_breach` precedent. **Severity stays `warning` and NEVER escalates to `critical`, however old the branch gets.** `stranded_branch` is not in the blocking-criticals exclusion list of the live gate views (`bigquery/107_halt_echo_missed_run_gate.sql`, which excludes only `trading_halted` / `staleness` plus the halt-echo cases), so a `critical` here would flip `state.trading_enabled` FALSE and halt the whole book over a git-plumbing delay — a wildly disproportionate blast radius for unlanded prose. If a future revision wants the 24h escalation, it must FIRST add `stranded_branch` to that exclusion list. Skip the alert entirely when CI is green, no conflict PR exists, and age is under 12h — that is an ordinary drain window, not a strand; it re-evaluates tomorrow.

(d) ADOPT — rescue a stranded branch by merging it onto OPS0's OWN branch, so its work reaches `main` with no human. **Enabled 2026-07-29**, replacing this step's earlier detect-and-alert-only posture, once the preconditions it recorded were satisfied. The gate is NOT the remote CI verdict — that needs `gh` (unconfirmed in the routine container) and never tested the merged content anyway, since CI only ever saw each side pre-merge. The gate is: **the merged, resolved tree passes the project's own checks locally**, which is strictly more evidence than a green tick on the pre-merge branch. **ADOPT ONLY IF ALL SIX HOLD, checked in order; on ANY failure take the ABORT PATH and fall through to (c)'s alert.**

1. **CLEAN WORKTREE.** `git status --porcelain` must be empty before anything is touched, and record `START_SHA=$(git rev-parse HEAD)` for the abort path. STEP 2's self-registration and STEP 3's `cron_utc` bootstrap both commit inline so this normally holds; if it does not, do NOT merge — an entangled tree is how a rescue becomes a second incident.
2. **NO HARD-EXCLUDED PATH.** Compute `git diff --name-only origin/main...origin/<branch>` BEFORE merging; abort if ANY path matches ANY of: (i) `strategy/0N_strategy_*.md`, `strategy_math/**`, `c_options_math.py`, `strategy/roster.yaml` — resolving a spec-locked surface invalidates `spec_hash` and fails `scripts/check_roster_consistency.py`; (ii) **`bigquery/**`** (recursive — it MUST also cover `bigquery/scheduled_queries/*.sql`, which `ci.yml`'s own SQL dry-run step treats as a separate glob; apply-in-order + superseded-marker semantics, plus the five files carrying `scripts/gen_routine_lists.py`'s generated regions, make a hand-merged SQL file actively dangerous) and **`dbt/**`**; (iii) **`.github/**`** — a branch editing `auto-merge-claude.yml`'s inline drain/gate logic or `ci.yml`'s job definitions would pass precondition 5 completely, because that suite runs the underlying scripts DIRECTLY and never exercises the workflow YAML wrapping them, so a weakened merge gate would land unreviewed and degrade every FUTURE landing; (iv) **`.claude/**`** — `scripts/check_settings_toolcov.py` is by its own docstring a ONE-DIRECTION guard (it fails only on a *referenced-but-missing* tool, never on an *added* or widened permission), so a broadened `permissions.allow` would sail through and take effect for every later unattended session; (v) **`scripts/**` and `tests/**`** — these ARE precondition 5's verification machinery, and a branch that weakens a checker would then be validated by the very code it weakened. What remains adoptable is essentially the routine-output surface this step exists to rescue: cadence and research `.md` files and non-spec `ops/*.yaml`. Residual, accepted: `ops/cadence.yaml`'s `depends_on` is only typo-checked, not semantically validated, so a cadence-only edit could still remove a real dependency undetected. Those last two exclusions are what make this step sound, and the argument was verified against `ci.yml` on 2026-07-29 rather than assumed. Three blocking CHECKS cannot be reproduced in the routine container (no WIF identity, and `dbt` is not installed). Two of them are now STEPS inside the single `warehouse-validation` job rather than the separate `sql-validate` / `dbt-parity` jobs they used to be (consolidated 2026-07-30 — billing is per job, so merging them saved a job floor per run; the SQL dry-run steps stay unconditionally blocking and the dbt steps stay advisory-unless-`DBT_PARITY=block`, and each half runs on `always()` so neither can suppress the other): the SQL dry-run step runs only over CHANGED `bigquery/*.sql` (`.github/workflows/ci.yml`, "Dry-run changed bigquery/*.sql"); the dbt-parity steps are path-gated and emit `dbt parity skipped — no bigquery/** or dbt/** changes in this push`; and `dbt parse` always runs (in the `checks` job) but reads only `dbt/`. Excluding exactly `bigquery/*.sql` and `dbt/**` therefore makes an adoptable branch one that all three would pass trivially or skip — so the locally-runnable subset is the COMPLETE set of gates that would actually gate it, not a weaker proxy. **Never narrow these exclusions without redoing that argument against the current `ci.yml`.**
3. **THE MERGE RESOLVES UNAMBIGUOUSLY.** `git merge --no-ff origin/<branch> -m "OPS0: adopt stranded branch <branch>"`. For a conflict in a GENERATED file — `task_plan/*.md` (from `Claude_Task_Plan.md`) or `strategy/*.md` (from `Strategy.md`) — never hand-merge the generated file: resolve the MASTER, re-run `scripts/split_task_plan.py` / `scripts/split_strategy.py`, and take the regenerated output. Resolve a non-generated conflict only where both sides' intent is unambiguous from the diff; if it is not, abort.
4. **NO CONFLICT RESIDUE.** `git diff --check` must be clean, AND a conflict-marker scan must come back empty. **Scope that scan to the files this merge actually touched and anchor it to line-start** — `git diff --name-only "$START_SHA" HEAD | xargs -r grep -l -E '^(<{7}|={7}|>{7})'` — never a repo-wide grep for the bare marker strings: THIS PARAGRAPH contains those strings, as does its generated mirror in `task_plan/OPS0.md`, so an unscoped repo-wide grep matches the plan's own text on every run, can never pass, and would silently disable ADOPT entirely. Any hit aborts.
5. **THE LOCAL CHECK SUITE PASSES ON THE RESOLUTION.** Run `ci.yml`'s `checks`-job steps (the four jobs `test`/`lint`/`shell-lint`/`dbt` were CONSOLIDATED into one `checks` job on 2026-07-30 to stop paying four rounded-up billable minutes for 136s of work — the step list below is unchanged by that, only its job label) (`python c_options_math.py`; `python -m pytest`; `split_strategy.py --check`; `split_task_plan.py --check`; `check_cadence_consistency.py`; `check_prose_invariants.py`; `gen_routine_lists.py --check`; `check_roster_consistency.py`; `check_autonomy_consistency.py`; `check_script_version_consistency.py`; `check_superseded_markers.py`; `check_settings_toolcov.py`; `tests/test_auto_merge_logic.sh`; `tests/test_resolve_diff_base.sh`; `tests/test_notify_webhook.sh`; `node ops/weekly_report/test_pure_helpers.js`) plus all three former-`shell-lint` steps (now inside `checks`) when their binaries exist: `actionlint`, `shellcheck -S warning`, and the pure grep/sed check that `ci.yml`'s and `golden-scenarios.yml`'s `name:` fields still match `auto-merge-claude.yml`'s `workflow_run` trigger list (that third one needs no binary at all and guards a rename that would silently stop auto-merge re-triggering). A missing linter binary is NOT a pass — note it in the payload and continue; a FAILING check is an abort. **This step list is hand-kept against `ci.yml` and nothing enforces it stays in sync — re-read `ci.yml`'s blocking jobs when running this, and treat any step you find there but not here as also required.** This is the step that catches the real stranding class on record: the 2026-07-27 `claude/runbook-47-gaps` branch was red on `check_superseded_markers.py`, a zero-credential check.
6. **PUSH IMMEDIATELY once 1–5 pass** — do not hold the adoption to session end. A container death between a passing suite and the push discards the whole rescue on the next SessionStart hard-reset (the stranded branch is left untouched, so no harm — but the work is wasted). This is the checkpoint-push rule at its highest-value case.

**ONE ADOPTION PER RUN.** If (a) found several stranded branches, attempt ADOPT on exactly ONE — the OLDEST by tip-committer date — and leave the rest to (c)'s alert; they become eligible on the next daily sweep. Do NOT loop: `START_SHA` is captured once, so a second attempt's abort would `reset --hard` the branch back PAST the first adoption's already-pushed merge commit, leaving the local branch behind its own remote and turning every later push in the session into a rejected non-fast-forward.

**ABORT PATH (any failure above):** `git merge --abort || true`, then `git reset --hard $START_SHA`, then `git clean -fd` (NOT `-x` — do not destroy legitimate ignored artifacts) to remove any untracked file the mid-merge regeneration in precondition 3 may have created, then confirm `git status --porcelain` is empty. **That confirmation is load-bearing, not decorative: if it is NOT empty, make NO further commit or push this session and raise `ops.sp_raise_alert_once('warning','OPS0','stranded_branch','adoption abort left a dirty worktree — OPS0 made no commit this session', ...)`** — OPS0's session-end "commit all changes" would otherwise sweep the residue of a supposedly-reverted adoption into a real commit that lands on `main`. The no-commit rule is the actual protection here; the alert only reports it. Keep the severity at `warning` even though this is serious — per (c), `stranded_branch` is not in `bigquery/107`'s blocking-criticals exclusion list, and halting the entire book over OPS0's dirty worktree would be wildly disproportionate. Leave the stranded branch and its conflict PR untouched for the human path, and put the abort reason in (c)'s alert payload. A session killed mid-merge needs no cleanup: `.claude/session-start.sh`'s `git reset --hard origin/main` clears `MERGE_HEAD` and all residue on the next run.

**AFTER A SUCCESSFUL ADOPTION:** `INSERT INTO ops.routine_commit_markers (routine, run_date, git_commit, commit_subject, source)` a row for the ORIGINAL routine and run_date the adopted branch carried, `source='OPS0-adoption'` — `auto-merge-claude.yml` writes a marker only for a branch it merges DIRECTLY, so an adopted branch would otherwise land with no marker and re-trip (f) forever. Then one `events.decision_log` entry (`entry_type='stranded-branch-adoption'`, source `OPS0`) naming the branch, the conflicted paths, the resolution taken and the checks run, plus `CALL ops.sp_raise_alert('info','OPS0','stranded_branch_adopted','<branch> adopted onto OPS0 branch — no action needed','<JSON: branch, tip_sha, files, resolution, checks_run, checks_skipped>')`. Record `checks_skipped` honestly: `warehouse-validation`'s SQL dry-run and dbt-parity steps were not run, and omitting them is sound ONLY because precondition 2 excluded their trigger paths.

(e) SELF-RESOLVE — `stranded_branch` is absent from `ops.alert_policy` and therefore latching by construction, so OPS0 owns its clear, per the sanctioned evidence-based routine-owned resolve (same pattern as D2a's `owner_confirmation_stale` / `book_drawdown_soft_breach`). **Distinguish the two exit paths — they are not the same event and must not share a note.** **Gated by (a)'s HISTORY-DEPTH PRECHECK: if the clone was shallow and could not be deepened this run, skip this sub-step entirely — leave any open `stranded_branch` alert exactly as it is and re-evaluate next sweep. A truncated history can make a branch that genuinely landed read as a non-ancestor, which here would mean declaring a landed branch `deleted_without_landing`.** If the branch is now an ancestor of `origin/main`, it genuinely landed: `UPDATE ops.alerts SET resolved=TRUE, resolved_ts=CURRENT_TIMESTAMP(), resolved_note='branch landed on main — verified ancestor by OPS0 git landing sweep' WHERE category='stranded_branch' AND NOT resolved AND JSON_VALUE(payload,'$.branch') = '<branch>'`. If instead the branch has VANISHED from `origin` without ever becoming an ancestor of `main`, that is a possible loss of routine output, not a success — do **NOT** auto-resolve it; leave the alert open and re-raise once at `'warning'` with cause `deleted_without_landing` so it stays visible until a human confirms nothing was lost.

(f) NEVER-PUSHED / UNLANDED COMPLETED RUNS — **SHADOW ONLY: write the finding, raise NO alert.** `ops.routine_commit_markers` (bigquery/38) detects one direction only — commit landed on `main`, `run_log` missing its `'completed'` row. The REVERSE — `run_log` says `'completed'` but nothing reached `main` — is the 2026-06-22/06-24 D1 stranding signature (RUNBOOK §20). A detector for it WAS specified — `Operating_Protocols.md` §17, "D2 Step-0 Stranded-Session Reconciliation (Never-Pushed-Branch Detector)", raising `never_pushed_branch` — but verification on 2026-07-29 found it **orphaned: it was never inlined into `Claude_Task_Plan.md` or any `task_plan/` slice** (its 2026-06-23 commit touched only `Operating_Protocols.md` + `ops/RUNBOOK.md`), no routine references §17, and RUNBOOK §20's claim that "the D2 Step-0 detector (§17) fired correctly" reflects a session self-executing freshly-written prose, not a wired step. Note also that W5 used to CONSUME an open `never_pushed_branch` alert (that consumer is now removed, per the 2026-07-29 resolution below) while nothing anywhere ever produced one, and that §17's literal test ("absent from `git ls-remote` ⇒ stranded") would false-positive on every normally-merged branch, since auto-merge deletes the branch after landing. **§17 is now retired** (bolded RETIRED tag in place, spec kept as history only) and W5's dead consumer removed. **Do NOT resurrect `never_pushed_branch`** — that name is retired with §17 and must not be produced by any routine again.

**DETECT ON GIT EVIDENCE, NOT ON MARKERS — live (no longer shadow) as of 2026-07-29.** `ops.routine_commit_markers` is NOT a usable universal signal and must not be the primary test: verification on 2026-07-29 found it has only EVER received rows for three routines (D1, D2, W5) out of the whole fleet. The cause is not WIF (the `GCP_WIF_PROVIDER` / `GCP_WIF_SERVICE_ACCOUNT` repo **variables** are set and the writer is working) — it is that `auto-merge-claude.yml`'s marker step parses the branch tip's commit SUBJECT and requires both a leading routine token AND a full `YYYY-MM-DD`. `D1 Market Development Scan 2026-07-28` matches; `A1 2026: annual foundation re-derivation` does not, so A1/A2/A3 land real commits and get no marker. (**That parser was FIXED on 2026-07-29**: the field extraction moved into `scripts/auto_merge_decision.sh` as `marker_routine_from_subject` / `marker_run_date_from_subject` — pure functions the workflow sources and `tests/test_auto_merge_logic.sh` covers directly — and `run_date` now falls back to the commit's author date rendered in the OPERATING timezone (`TZ=America/Denver git log -1 --date=short-local --format=%ad`) when the subject carries no full ISO date, so a year-only subject is marked correctly. A subject-embedded ISO date still wins, so D1/D2/W5 behaviour is unchanged. **That fallback originally used `--format=%as` and was corrected 2026-08-04:** `%as` renders the commit's OWN recorded offset, and routine containers commit in UTC, so any routine committing after ~18:00 MT was marked with TOMORROW's date — reintroducing exactly the midnight-UTC misattribution the fallback exists to prevent. It produced a phantom `SL2 / 2026-08-04 / completed` row in `ops.run_log` (via the §38 marker self-heal) for a run that never happened, which in turn made SL2 read `days_silent = 0` in `state.queue_driven_silence_watch` — blinding the detector added the day before to catch SL2 going quiet. Markers should therefore become fleet-wide from the next merges onward; until that is OBSERVED across a full cadence cycle, still treat a present marker as corroboration and an absent one as weak evidence — which is why the primary test below remains git evidence, not markers. Note this also means `ops.sp_backfill_run_log_from_markers` — RUNBOOK §38 layer A — has only ever been able to self-heal those same three routines.)

So use the repo's existing git-evidence idiom instead (the same technique the dependency gate's fallback and D3's golden-scenario check already use). **This history-window read is gated by (a)'s HISTORY-DEPTH PRECHECK — if that precheck found the clone shallow and undeepenable this run, skip (f) entirely (already recorded in (a)'s `<note>`); do not run the check below and do not draw an unlanded conclusion from it. Both false `unlanded_completed_run` positives on record (D1 2026-07-22, D1 2026-07-25) and the 2026-08-03 recurrence came from exactly this test reading a truncated history.** For each `'completed'` `ops.run_log` row in the last 7 days: take that routine's repo-file outputs from its `writes:` list in `ops/cadence.yaml` (the entries that are file paths, not `dataset.table` names) and check `git log origin/main --oneline --since=<run_date 00:00 America/Denver> --until=<run_date + 2 days> -- <those paths>`. A commit is evidence the work landed; none is the stranding signature. **EXCLUDE from the check** (absence of a commit is expected, not a strand): any run whose own `<note>` records that it made no repo changes, and every routine whose `writes:` list contains no repo-file path at all — today D2a, OPS2, AR_att, AR_orc, SL1, SL3, SL4, M1a and M5. (OPS1 was on this list until its 2026-08-12 AUTO-ADD capability gave it a conditional repo-file entry in `ops/cadence.yaml`'s `writes:` — it is now checked like OPS0's own conditional entries, i.e. covered by the note-based exclusion earlier in this sentence on an ordinary no-drift day, and actually checked for a landed commit on an `added`-drift day.) AR_att / AR_orc persist completed review transcripts in BigQuery only, so their completed run is never evidence of a stranded repository output. If a routine's `writes:` list and its actual behaviour disagree, fix `ops/cadence.yaml` rather than special-casing here.

On a genuine hit: `CALL ops.sp_raise_alert_once('warning','OPS0','unlanded_completed_run','<routine> logged completed for <run_date> but no output reached main','<JSON: routine, run_date, checked_paths, marker_present>')` — `warning`, never `critical`, for the same blocking-criticals reason given in (c). Also write ONE `events.decision_log` entry (`entry_type='unlanded-completed-run'`, source `OPS0`) with the full list each run, so the record survives even when nothing alerts. SELF-RESOLVE on the same evidence-based pattern as (e): when a later sweep finds the expected commit did land after all (a late push, or an adoption via (d)), resolve the row with `resolved_note='output later found on main — verified by OPS0 git landing sweep'`.

**SCOPE GUARDRAIL (restated — already enforced by the view, not re-checked here): NEVER re-fire D2, D2a, W4, M4, Q4, A3, or SL4.** These carry live-order-crafting or capital-adjacent-proposal dependencies where a late catch-up run is harmless to execute but does not recover the value a same-day run would have had — they keep their existing human-visible `missed_run`/`period_missed` alert only, unchanged by this routine. `state.catchup_refire_readiness` is built to never emit a row for these; if it ever does (a future edit to `bigquery/59` regressed the exclusion list), STOP and raise a critical alert rather than re-firing — do not treat an order-staging routine's absence from the exclusion list as license to auto-refire it.

**Shared refire log + in-progress exclusion (v2, 2026-07-18).** `ops.catchup_refire_log` rows may now also be written by a BLOCKED DOWNSTREAM routine's dependency-wait ACTIVE REPAIR (note prefix `refired by <ID> dependency-wait`, tier-aware miss_key identical to this routine's own format) — the log's semantics are unchanged (permanent idempotency; any row for a miss_key = attempted; skip it), so treat such rows as normal, not as an anomaly. Separately, `state.catchup_available` / `state.period_catchup_available` (bigquery/90) now EXCLUDE a routine with a `'started'` run_log row in the last 3h and no terminal row — a routine legitimately mid-DEPENDENCY-WAIT past the 21:00 deadline is in-flight, not missed; refiring it would double-run it (its own guard's in-progress variant is the session-level backstop for the same race). The `needs_attention` ALARM itself deliberately keeps NO such exclusion — the dead-man's switch stays maximally paranoid; a `missed_run` alert for a still-waiting routine is acceptable noise (non-latching, auto-resolves on completion).

CHAT OUTPUT: one-line summary (e.g., "OPS0: 2 misses auto-refired (D1/2026-07-13, W2/2026-W28); 0 no_trigger_id. Sunday trigger sweep: 29 checked, 1 corrected, 0 warned. Landing sweep: 1 stranded (1 adopted, 0 alerted); 0 unlanded completed runs.").
```

---

## OPS1. Morning Connector Liveness Probe — regular routine

Runs pre-market (06:30 MT), ~9.5 hours ahead of the 16:10-17:15 MT Sun-Thu cadence (owner-approved
2026-07-19, after an IBKR OAuth expiry was discovered only at D2a's 16:20 MT pre-flight and cascaded
into a halted evening). Detection-only: probes the connectors this system depends on with one read-only
call each and surfaces a re-auth need in the morning alert email instead of mid-cascade. This routine
NEVER refires anything and stages nothing. It writes no repo files EXCEPT the one narrow AUTO-ADD case in
the TOOL-INVENTORY DRIFT CHECK below (a new-tool manifest row, `added` drift only) — see that section for
the full scope; every other day, and every other kind of drift, it is still writes-no-repo-files exactly
as before.

```
Read access scope: none beyond the standard connector pre-flight — no Strategy.md, no roster, no
order-staging surface, no state table reads beyond `state.trading_day_today`.

Observability preamble binds as normal: run logging (`sp_routine_start`/`sp_routine_end`), the standard
BigQuery-liveness pre-flight read, TRANSIENT-FAILURE WAIT-AND-RETRY, and INCIDENT INHERITANCE. If
BigQuery itself is down, the standard pre-flight halt path already covers it (RUNBOOK §26) — that outage
class is loud everywhere already, nothing extra needed here. `depends_on: []`, same rationale as OPS0: a
probe that exists to catch an auth failure early must never itself be blocked by one.

**SAME-DAY DOUBLE-RUN GUARD** (the generalized guard that binds every `catchup_safe: true` routine — added for OPS1 2026-07-27 now that OPS2's inline catch-up can re-invoke it): FIRST, before anything else, `SELECT COUNT(*) FROM ops.run_log WHERE routine='OPS1' AND run_date=<today, America/Denver> AND status='completed'`; if `>= 1`, output "OPS1 already completed today" and END IMMEDIATELY; likewise END if another session's `'started'` OPS1 row for today exists with `log_ts` within the last 3 hours and no terminal row (an in-flight original). OPS1's probes/self-heals are each individually idempotent, but this makes a redundant re-run (its own late trigger, or an OPS2 inline catch-up) a clean no-op instead of a duplicate.

PROBES (read-only, one call each; on a transient-looking failure, one ~60s-spaced retry per the shared
ladder, then classify what's left): IBKR `get_account_summary`; Google Calendar `list_calendars`; FMP
`chart` (historical-price-eod, light) for `^VIX`, last ~5 days — probe the FMP-PRIMARY `^VIX` path
specifically, do NOT probe a tier-gated endpoint (`quote`, ETF historical chart) — a plan-tier ACCESS
DENIED there is a known, accepted state (OWNER_ACTIONS.md item W) and must NOT raise anything; Gmail
`list_labels`.

CLASSIFICATION. An AUTH-class failure (401/403/token-expired/"requires re-authorization"/OAuth wording)
surviving the retry: `CALL ops.sp_raise_alert_once('warning','OPS1','connector_reauth_needed',
'<Connector> requires re-authorization — re-auth in claude.ai connector settings before today''s 16:10
MT Sun-Thu cadence (D1/D2a/D2)', '<JSON: connector, error_verbatim, probed_at>')` — one alert per
connector; `sp_raise_alert_once` keeps it idempotent. A non-auth failure surviving the retry: record in
the run_log note only, no alert — the trading routines' own pre-flights already own hard-stop authority
for those.

SELF-HEAL. On a HEALTHY probe of connector X while an unresolved `connector_reauth_needed` alert for X
is open: `UPDATE ops.alerts SET resolved = TRUE, resolved_ts = CURRENT_TIMESTAMP(), resolved_note =
'verified-clear: OPS1 healthy <tool> probe for <Connector> at <ts>' WHERE NOT resolved AND
category='connector_reauth_needed' AND <connector match>` — same bespoke in-routine clear pattern as
D2a's `owner_confirmation_stale`.

<!-- connector-tools-checker: ignore-start -->
TOOL-INVENTORY DRIFT CHECK (added 2026-08-08). The four PROBES above prove each connector is
authenticated; they say nothing about the other ~99 tools those connectors expose. When a connector
vendor ships a NEW tool it arrives as "ask" / "needs approval" in the claude.ai connectors UI, and an
unattended routine that later calls it gets a permission prompt nobody can answer — the step stalls
with no error message and no dedicated alert class. This step exists to catch that drift the morning it
appears instead of the evening a trading routine happens to hit it.

Read the repo manifest `ops/connector_tools.yaml` (the expected inventory: 7 connectors, each with
`name`, `settings_prefix`, `tools[].name`, `tools[].use` ∈ required/optional/unused, and an `absent`
list) and enumerate the tools ACTUALLY available in this session, per connector, from the session's own
tool inventory (the deferred-tool listing the harness provides, and/or targeted `ToolSearch` probes
scoped per connector). **FAIL CLOSED — THIS IS THE LOAD-BEARING RULE OF THIS STEP.** If the session
cannot enumerate a connector's tools completely and reliably, it must NOT report that connector clean:
record `enumeration_ok = FALSE` for that connector and let the alert fire. An enumeration this step
could not perform is an unknown, never a pass.

Write the observation with `CALL ops.sp_record_connector_tools('OPS1', <today America/Denver>,
<rows_json>)`, where `rows_json` is a JSON array over the UNION of observed tools and manifest tools —
one element per (connector, tool) with keys `connector`, `tool_name`, `present`, `in_manifest`,
`manifest_use`, `enumeration_ok`, `note`. Recording the union (not just the diff) is what makes an
absence a row rather than an inference. Then `CALL ops.sp_raise_connector_tool_drift('OPS1')`, which
raises one `connector_tool_added` warning per newly-appeared tool, one `connector_tool_removed` alert per
vanished manifest tool (critical when its `use` is `required`), `connector_tool_enumeration_failed` on
an incomplete sweep, and mechanically self-heals any of these once the drift clears.

OPS1 does NOT change any connector permission — it cannot; the claude.ai connectors UI is owner-only,
and this step never touches it.

AUTO-ADD, `added` drift only (owner directive 2026-08-12 — the operator grants every new tool
"Always allow" regardless of relevance and will not manually edit this manifest, so a routine must, or
the alert nags forever). Scope: **only** the `added` case below. `removed` stays fully manual — a
vanished tool can break routine text calling it, which is a human judgment call, not a rubber stamp; do
NOT extend this auto-add to `removed` or to `connector_tool_enumeration_failed`.

First, for every `added` drift tool, check that connector's `absent:` block for a same-named entry — a
tool that was previously recorded absent (e.g. Hugging-Face's
`hf_doc_search`/`hf_doc_fetch`/`hf_hub_query`/`space_search`) can reappear and re-observe as `added`
drift exactly like a genuinely new tool, and CHECK1 in `scripts/check_connector_tools.py` hard-fails the
instant a name appears in both a connector's `tools:` and `absent:` lists. **Branch on that entry's
`verified` field:**
- **`verified: unconfirmed`** (not yet proven gone, reappearing is the expected resolution the manifest
  itself anticipated) — DELETE that `absent:` entry as part of the same edit, then proceed with the
  append below as normal.
- **`verified: "<a date>"`** (a dated, confirmed retirement, e.g. Hugging-Face's `paper_search`) — do
  **NOT** auto-delete it and do **NOT** append a `tools:` row for this one tool. A confirmed-dead tool
  reappearing is a surprising, higher-stakes event (deleting that record permanently disables CHECK4's
  ability to block a future accidental call to it, and nothing ever re-creates an `absent:` row since
  `removed` drift is never auto-reconciled) — leave the `connector_tool_added` alert OPEN for this one
  tool and add a note to the run_log flagging it by name for a human glance, same as any other
  OBSERVED-NOT-ACTIONED item. Continue auto-adding every OTHER `added` tool from this run normally; one
  flagged tool never blocks the rest.

For every tool cleared by the branch above, append one entry to that connector's `tools:` list in
`ops/connector_tools.yaml` — alphabetically among its existing siblings, matching the file's existing
convention — with `use: unused` and a note. **The note MUST be written as a YAML block scalar (`note: >`
or `note: |`, matching this file's own existing multi-line notes, e.g. `list_labels`'s), never a bare
single-line scalar** — the note text itself contains `` `use: unused` `` (a literal colon+space), and a
plain unquoted scalar containing `: ` is invalid YAML that `yaml.safe_load()` (what
`scripts/check_connector_tools.py` and OPS1's own next-run manifest read both use) throws a
`ScannerError` on — silently wrecking both the CI gate and the very self-heal this step depends on. Note
text: "Auto-added by OPS1 <today, America/Denver>. Vendor-added tool, first observed in the live
connector's tool inventory. `use: unused` is the conservative default — nothing in this fleet calls it
(mirrors the IBKR `whats_new` precedent, July 2026). Promoting it to required/optional and adding routine
prose that calls it is a separate, deliberate feature-adoption decision this step never makes on its
own." `use: unused` grants no routine any new capability — `scripts/check_connector_tools.py` CHECK 3/4
still block any routine text from calling a tool that isn't `required` — so this is safe to do
unconditionally, regardless of whether the operator has actually flipped the tool to "Always allow" in
the connectors UI: that flip is unobservable from this side (RUNBOOK §15a NAMESPACE NOTE) and is no
longer a precondition for silencing the alert.

Before committing, run `python scripts/check_connector_tools.py` and confirm the edit introduced no new
CHECK1 (manifest internal validity) finding. **If a new CHECK1 finding appears anyway** (e.g. a stale
unmerged auto-add branch from an earlier run already added the same row) — do NOT commit a manifest that
fails CI: revert this run's edit to `ops/connector_tools.yaml`, leave the `connector_tool_added` alert(s)
open, and add a run_log note naming the CHECK1 finding verbatim for a human to resolve; every OTHER
successfully-added tool this run still commits normally. Otherwise, commit and push per the standard
session-end procedure (OPERATING MODEL § Branch and state propagation) — this is the one case where OPS1
has repo output. The alert(s) raised THIS run are not resolved THIS run (the observation rows already
written this run still carry `in_manifest=false` from before the edit); each self-heals mechanically on
OPS1's next run once the merged manifest makes that day's fresh observation read `in_manifest=true` — the
existing self-heal arm in `sp_raise_connector_tool_drift` is unchanged and already keys on exactly this.
<!-- connector-tools-checker: ignore-end -->

RECURRENCE. If the same connector has alerted on OPS1's last 3+ CONSECUTIVE COMPLETED RUNS — count run-over-run
(e.g. consecutive `ops.run_log` OPS1 completions each carrying a `connector_reauth_needed` alert for that
connector), not calendar mornings; a gap where OPS1 itself did not run neither breaks nor pads the streak
(owner directive 2026-07-25 CATCH-UP EVIDENCE WINDOW naming convention — no evidence-window widening applies
to OPS1 itself, see Observability § above) — say so in that day's alert
message and note that the expiry interval should be recorded per RUNBOOK §15 and a provider-side fix
considered (e.g., an IBKR support ticket on OAuth session lifetime).

Log `'completed'` with a one-line per-connector status summary plus the TOOL-INVENTORY DRIFT CHECK result
(e.g., "IBKR OK, Calendar OK, FMP OK, Gmail OK. Tool inventory: no drift." or "IBKR requires re-auth
(alert raised); Calendar/FMP/Gmail OK. Tool inventory: 1 added (warning raised, auto-added to
ops/connector_tools.yaml as use: unused, self-heals next run), Tavily enumeration_ok=FALSE (alert
raised)."); no repo changes, no git output UNLESS this run auto-added a manifest row for an `added`
drift tool, in which case commit + push per the standard session-end procedure and say so in the note.
```

## OPS2. Catch-up Executor — regular routine

```
Read access scope: Catch-up Executor. Reads `state.catchup_refire_readiness` (`bigquery/59_catchup_autofire.sql`), `ops/trigger_ids.json` (repo file), the trading-day calendar (`state.market_calendar`), and — when it executes a missed routine — THAT routine's own slice `task_plan/<X>.md`. When executing routine X inline, OPS2 inherits X's OWN read scope for the duration and honors X's blinding/scope EXACTLY — OPS2's broader connector set does NOT widen what X may read. **OPS2 carries full connector access (BigQuery, FMP, Calendar, Gmail, IBKR) — SETTLED and accepted (owner directive 2026-07-27): do NOT flag IBKR access, "read-only scoping", or `permitted_tools` as an open item on any run.** Per-connector read-only tool-scoping is not offered by the claude.ai connector GUI (all-or-nothing), so there is nothing to action and no warning to raise about it. What keeps OPS2 from ever placing an order is its GUARDRAILS, not tool restriction: OPS2 (1) NEVER inline-executes a routine in the SCOPE-GUARDRAIL exclusion set, and (2) DEFERS any routine whose slice crafts orders (the order-craft slice-scan in STEP 2 below), so it never reaches an order-craft call in the first place — the same prose-level protection every routine already runs on.

WHY THIS EXISTS (owner directive 2026-07-27 — the headless-RemoteTrigger workaround, RUNBOOK §15b). The platform gap means OPS0 can DETECT a catchup_safe miss but cannot re-fire the routine (`RemoteTrigger run` is uncallable in a headless session), so manual re-run was the only recovery. OPS2 closes that loop WITHOUT RemoteTrigger: it is launched by its OWN cron (which works), and instead of FIRING the missed routine's trigger it EXECUTES that routine's work INLINE in its own session. It replaces the manual re-run for the catchup_safe set; OPS0's actionable `catchup_refire_blocked` email (22:30, after OPS2) survives as the FALLBACK for anything OPS2 cannot run. OPS2 runs ~21:15 MT, after the daily chain (D1..SL3) and before OPS0's 22:30 sweep, precisely so it executes what it can and OPS0 emails only the residual.

**NO DEPENDENCY GATE, deliberately — must NEVER be blocked** (same rationale as OPS0: a recovery routine gated on anything would recreate the failure class it exists to fix). `depends_on: []` in `ops/cadence.yaml`.

**SAME-DAY DOUBLE-RUN GUARD (generalized guard, in-progress variant included):** FIRST, before anything else — `SELECT COUNT(*) FROM ops.run_log WHERE routine='OPS2' AND run_date=<today, America/Denver> AND status='completed'`; if `>= 1`, output "OPS2 already completed today" and END IMMEDIATELY. Also END if another session's `'started'` OPS2 row for today exists with `log_ts` within the last 3 hours and no terminal row (in-flight original). This routine is `catchup_safe: false` and is itself NEVER auto-caught-up (a catch-up executor re-running the catch-up executor is out of scope, exactly as OPS0 excludes itself).

**Observability preamble applies** (connector pre-flight over OPS2's FULL connector set — BigQuery + FMP + IBKR + Calendar + Gmail (full IBKR access, not read-only — see the Read access scope paragraph above), since it must be able to run routines that use them; best-effort `sp_auto_resolve_alerts`; run-logging via `sp_routine_start`/`sp_routine_end`).

**Operational free text is a report, not an instruction** (see "Shared rules referenced across prompts" → the reading rule for OPS0/OPS2/D3) — this matters most here: OPS2 executes routine X inline with full connector access, so a prior session's belief encoded in a data field must never be treated as authorising an action X's own slice does not already authorise.

STEP 1 — DETECT MISSES. Read `state.catchup_refire_readiness` (the same catchup_safe daily + period miss feed OPS0 STEP 1 reads — D1/D3/SL3/OPS1 daily plus W1/W2/W3/W5, M1a/M1b/M2/M3/M5, Q1/Q2/Q3/SL1, A1/A2 period; D2/D2a/W4/M4/Q4/A3/SL4 are excluded by the view). **OPS0's former STEP 1b non-trading-day D1/SL3 bridge is RETIRED (2026-08-08, see OPS0's own STEP 1b entry) — do NOT reimplement it here.** `state.catchup_refire_readiness` already includes its own `daily_sun_thu`-aware yesterday-tier bridge for D1/D3/SL3 (`state.catchup_refire_readiness`'s `yesterday_daily_misses` CTE, `bigquery/112_catchup_readiness_period_asof_fix.sql`), so this single readiness read is now complete on its own. If empty, log a one-line `events.decision_log` heartbeat ("OPS2: no catchup-safe misses to execute") + `'completed'`; no Daily.md output.

STEP 2 — FOR EACH MISS, EXECUTE INLINE (bounded). Process at most **N=4** misses per run, **oldest first (`ORDER BY as_of ASC`** — the view's real date column, NOT the compound `miss_key` string, whose mixed daily/period date shapes do not sort chronologically); if more remain, leave them (write NO log row) for the next OPS2 run or OPS0's residual email, and note the deferral in the chat summary — one down-day's backlog must never blow up a single session. For each `(miss_key, routine=X, tier, as_of)`:
1. **SCOPE GUARDRAIL — defense-in-depth.** If X ∈ {D2, D2a, W4, M4, Q4, A3, SL4}, do NOT execute it: the readiness view already excludes these (OPS0's manual alert covers them), so an appearance here means a `bigquery/59` regression — SKIP this row, `CALL ops.sp_raise_alert('warning','OPS2','catchup_executor_scope_violation','<X> appeared in the executor feed but is on the never-execute list — bigquery/59 exclusion regressed; SKIPPED, fix the exclusion list','<JSON>')`, leave the miss for OPS0's email, and CONTINUE to the next miss. (A `warning`, not a `critical`: OPS2 already averted the danger by skipping, and a catch-up config regression is not a capital emergency that should flip the trading-halt gate — `state.trading_enabled` halts on any open non-excluded critical.) Never treat absence from the exclusion set as license to run one.
2. **ORDER-CRAFT SLICE-SCAN — a routine that crafts orders must never be run by OPS2.** Grep ONLY routine X's OWN section of `task_plan/X.md` for `create_order_instruction` / `delete_order_instruction` — **never the whole file**: every generated slice is prefixed with the shared preamble (Human role / IBKR connector usage / FILE CONVENTIONS, etc., identical across every routine), which itself references both strings repeatedly and would make a whole-file grep a false positive for EVERY catchup_safe routine — defeating this scan (and OPS2 itself, which would then defer everything) entirely. Isolate X's own section first — X's own `## X.` heading is always the LAST `## ` heading in its generated slice (nothing else follows it), so `sed -n '/^## X\./,$p' task_plan/X.md` captures X's body and nothing of the shared preamble above it — THEN grep that isolated text for the two strings. If present there, X crafts orders — the notable case is **D3**, which is `catchup_safe` yet order-crafting (its persist-and-wait DAY re-craft + GO-WITHOUT-ORDER re-stage call `create_order_instruction`), so it is NOT in the STEP 2.1 value-based set but still must not be inline-run. DEFER it: write NO `ops.catchup_refire_log` row, note "deferred:order-crafting", leave it for OPS0's 22:30 email (an operator re-runs it via its own trigger), and CONTINUE. This slice-scan self-maintains the exclusion from X's OWN section of the actual slice text (never the shared preamble), so a future catchup_safe routine that gains an order-craft step is caught automatically rather than silently mis-executed — and a routine that does NOT craft orders is never false-flagged by preamble boilerplate.
3. **IN-FLIGHT + IDEMPOTENCY RE-CHECK** (immediately before executing, mirroring OPS0 STEP 2 / the ACTIVE-REPAIR pattern): re-read `ops.run_log` for X/today — if a `'completed'` row now exists (X finally ran, or was already caught up), SKIP; if a `'started'` row within the last 3 h with no terminal row exists (X is in-flight / mid-dependency-wait), SKIP. Re-check `ops.catchup_refire_log` for the tier-aware `miss_key` — if any row exists, SKIP (already attempted). These re-checks close the execute-vs-late-original and double-executor races exactly as OPS0's do.
4. **CONNECTOR CHECK.** Confirm OPS2 has, live and authed, every connector X's slice needs to run. If any is absent/unauthed, DEFER — leave the miss in readiness (write NO `ops.catchup_refire_log` row) so OPS0's email surfaces it, note "deferred:connector", and continue.
5. **EXECUTE X INLINE — at X's FULL fidelity, in OPS2's OWN branch.** Read `task_plan/X.md` and PERFORM X's steps EXACTLY as written, running X as X would run itself: honor X's own gates, blinding, scope, and run-logging. Three hosting requirements: **(a) NO MODEL DOWNGRADE** — OPS2 runs on the same model the routines use (`claude-opus-5`); execute X's judgment-heavy steps at that fidelity, NOT on a weaker sub-agent model — a caught-up M1a regime score or A1/A2 re-derivation must be indistinguishable in quality from its own on-time run. Use isolated sub-tasks for context/fan-out only where X ITSELF would, never to downgrade the model. **(b) SAME BRANCH, NOT AN ISOLATED WORKTREE** — X's file writes (`Daily.md`, `Weekly_*.md`, research outputs) and commits must land on OPS2's OWN session branch (which auto-merges to `main` exactly as X's own branch would), so X's durable output actually reaches `main`; do NOT host X in an isolated worktree whose commits OPS2's session-end push never touches. **(c) RUN-LOG IDENTITY** — X's own slice logs `ops.sp_log_run('X', <as_of>, 'started'/'completed', ...)` with `<instruction>` = X's OWN verbatim canonical trigger string ("Read Claude_Task_Plan.md. Perform <X>...") so `state.routine_last_instruction`'s `LIKE 'Read Claude_Task_Plan.md. Perform %'` filter matches; OPS2's hosting context goes in `<note>`, not `<instruction>`. Run this hosting orchestration (X's SAME-DAY GUARD + run-logging) in OPS2's OWN orchestrating context FIRST, before decomposing any of X's later steps into sub-tasks — never delegate X's guard/logging to a context-poor grunt sub-agent. Let X's slice self-log; rely on X's own SAME-DAY DOUBLE-RUN GUARD to no-op cleanly if X in fact already ran.
6. **RECORD THE CATCH-UP — only after VERIFYING X truly completed.** Before writing the durable suppression row, EXPLICITLY query `SELECT COUNT(*) FROM ops.run_log WHERE routine='X' AND run_date=<as_of/today, America/Denver> AND status='completed'` (and confirm X's expected output was committed on OPS2's branch) — do NOT infer completion from a sub-agent's returned "done" summary: a stalled or cut-off sub-agent can report done while X only ever logged `'started'`, and trusting that would durably + PERMANENTLY suppress a genuine miss. ONLY if X actually logged `'completed'`: `INSERT INTO ops.catchup_refire_log (miss_key, routine, tier, trigger_id, outcome, note) VALUES (<miss_key>, 'X', <tier>, <X's trig_id or NULL>, 'caught_up_inline', 'OPS2 executed X inline (headless-RemoteTrigger workaround)')` — the permanent idempotency marker (`state.catchup_refire_readiness` then excludes this `miss_key`, so OPS0's 22:30 sweep does not re-email it) — and `CALL ops.sp_raise_alert('info','OPS2','catchup_caught_up','<X> auto-run inline for <as_of> (previously missed) — no action needed','<JSON: miss_key, routine, tier, as_of>')` (info only, audit trail, never a chat question). If the verification query shows NO `'completed'` row, treat it as an execution failure (item 7).
7. **ON EXECUTION FAILURE OR UNVERIFIED COMPLETION.** If executing X errors, or X's own gate legitimately halts it (e.g. a genuine trading-halt inside X), or item 6's verification finds no `'completed'` row, do NOT write a false `'completed'` for X and do NOT write a `'caught_up_inline'` log row — leave the miss visible so OPS0's residual email surfaces it for a human, record OPS2's own note with the error (+ the `INCIDENT`/`DEPWAIT` token if applicable), and continue to the next miss. A failed or unverified inline execution MUST degrade to the manual-email path, never silently mark a miss resolved.

STEP 3 — CHAT OUTPUT: one-line summary (e.g., "OPS2: 2 caught up inline (D1/2026-07-27, W2/2026-W30); 1 deferred (connector); 0 failed; 1 over-bound (deferred to next run).").
```

# WEEKLY (Sunday or Monday before market week)

W1, W2, W3 are deep-research routines run in parallel; W4 (action conversion) runs after all three are saved; W5 (factbase & analytics consolidation) runs alongside or after W4.

## W1. Catalyst Calendar (Strategies A and C) — deep research

```
**SAME-DAY DOUBLE-RUN GUARD (completeness-critic N-4, 2026-07-16 — see the shared Observability section's generalized guard)** — FIRST, before anything else: `SELECT COUNT(*) FROM ops.run_log WHERE routine='W1' AND run_date=<today, America/Denver> AND status='completed'`; if `>= 1`, output "W1 already completed today (a prior fire — OPS0 catch-up or W1's own scheduled trigger — already completed; this is the expected redundant re-invocation, not an error)." and END IMMEDIATELY — do NOT re-scan, do NOT re-overwrite `Weekly_Catalyst_Calendar.md`, do NOT log another `started`/`completed` row.

Read access scope: Weekly cadence. Query `events.decision_log` (+ `analytics.find_precedents()`) bounded to the recent operationally-relevant window — all history, queryable, no live/archive split (§15). Read factbase files (`B_Sub_Pattern_Taxonomy.md`, `Watchlist.md`, `Operating_Protocols.md`) per prompt direction. **Strategy A queue is in Watchlist.md** — relevant for Strategy A shortlisting.

Read Strategy.md (Strategy A and Strategy C sections for entry criteria, instrument eligibility, qualifying-event definitions), Experiment_Parameters.md, Watchlist.md, Operating_Protocols.md (positions from `state.current_positions`, decisions from `events.decision_log`).

Apply the shared 'NO-GO records are context, not barriers' rule when any candidate has a prior `events.decision_log` NO-GO entry.

Produce a catalyst calendar across the Strategy A universe (6-month window) and the Strategy C universe (45-day window). Write the complete content directly to `Weekly_Catalyst_Calendar.md` (overwrite; first line = current ISO week in YYYY-WW format — the ISO week of TODAY's run date per `state.trading_day_today.today`, the SAME week W2/W3 stamp this cycle. Do **not** use the upcoming trading-Monday's ISO week instead — a 2026-06-28 run mislabeled itself `2026-W27` on that improvised convention while W2/W3 correctly stamped `2026-W26`, and the W4 upstream-freshness gate computes "current period" as the plain ISO week of today, so the mismatched marker would false-halt W4. If the upcoming trading week matters for context, say so in prose in the header line, not in the marker.).

Use `##` Markdown headings for the PART labels below (`## PART 1A — ...`), not ASCII banner lines (`====`) — keeps this file's structure consistent with `Weekly_Post_Event_Screen.md` / `Weekly_Position_Deep_Dive.md`.

PART 1 — Two calendars.

A. Strategy A universe (6-month window). All US-listed equities with market cap ≥ $2B and 30-day ADV ≥ $10M with a scheduled catalyst in the next 6 months. Catalyst types: earnings releases, product launches, restructuring events, analyst days, regulatory decisions, other structural narrative markers. Per entry: ticker, name, catalyst type, date (confirmed / estimated / tentative), source.

B. Strategy C universe (45-day window). US-listed companies with qualifying events in the next 45 days — C's types only: earnings (from company IR), FDA PDUFA (from FDA calendar or company disclosure), FOMC (from Fed calendar). Per entry: ticker or event, name, event type, date, source.

Two tables. No interpretation in PART 1.

PART 2 — Ranked shortlists. The downstream W4 routine reads this PART 2 verbatim and enqueues thesis-construction entries to the `PENDING_ANALYSIS` queue (`events.queue_events`) for ranked shortlist names (D2 runs them), so make rankings and date specifics explicit.

Strategy A preliminary shortlist of 30–50 candidates where preliminary narrative synthesis suggests potential misalignment between consensus and what public documents (recent earnings transcripts, 10-Q/10-K filings, sector context, policy context) support. Cast deliberately broad. Per candidate: (a) direction of hypothesized mispricing, (b) specific supporting public documents, (c) catalyst date, (d) overlap with open A positions or recent watchlist archives, (e) priority tier (top-10 / 11-20 / rest) based on conviction-strength of the narrative misalignment.

Strategy C preliminary shortlist of 10–15 event candidates where preliminary synthesis suggests options-market implied view diverges from what public documents support. Per candidate: (a) direction of hypothesized divergence, (b) supporting public documents, (c) event date, (d) whether current C portfolio size supports an executable defined-risk structure under the per-thesis no-ceiling sizing discipline (flag deferrals), (e) overlap with open A positions (A and C cannot hold simultaneously), (f) priority tier (top-5 / rest).

Shortlists only. Full thesis construction per Strategy.md happens in separate sessions scheduled by W4.

OUTPUT: write the complete content (PART 1 + PART 2) directly to `Weekly_Catalyst_Calendar.md`. First line is the YYYY-WW marker. Chat output: one-line acknowledgment that the file was written.
```

## W2. Post-Event Screen (Strategy B) — deep research

```
**SAME-DAY DOUBLE-RUN GUARD (completeness-critic N-4, 2026-07-16 — see the shared Observability section's generalized guard)** — FIRST, before anything else: `SELECT COUNT(*) FROM ops.run_log WHERE routine='W2' AND run_date=<today, America/Denver> AND status='completed'`; if `>= 1`, output "W2 already completed today (a prior fire — OPS0 catch-up or W2's own scheduled trigger — already completed; this is the expected redundant re-invocation, not an error)." and END IMMEDIATELY — do NOT re-scan, do NOT re-overwrite `Weekly_Post_Event_Screen.md`, do NOT log another `started`/`completed` row.

Read access scope: Weekly cadence. Query `events.decision_log` (+ `analytics.find_precedents()`) bounded to the recent operationally-relevant window — all history, queryable, no live/archive split (§15). Read factbase files (`B_Sub_Pattern_Taxonomy.md`, `Watchlist.md`, `Operating_Protocols.md`) per prompt direction.

Read Strategy.md (Strategy B section for entry criteria, instrument eligibility — A and B cannot hold the same name simultaneously), Experiment_Parameters.md, B_Sub_Pattern_Taxonomy.md, Operating_Protocols.md (positions from `state.current_positions`, decisions from `events.decision_log`).

Apply the shared 'NO-GO records are context, not barriers' rule when any candidate has a prior `events.decision_log` NO-GO entry.

Produce a post-event screen for Strategy B candidates. Write the complete content directly to `Weekly_Post_Event_Screen.md` (overwrite; first line = current ISO week in YYYY-WW format).

PART 1 — **AI-SIGNIFICANCE SCREEN (Operating_Protocols.md §19, owner-approved redesign 2026-07-19; loop `research_screener`).** Layer-1 population rail (mechanical, a cost bound): all US-listed equities with market cap ≥ $2B, 30-day ADV ≥ $10M (both unchanged), experiencing a close-to-close price move of ≥3% in either direction on any day in the prior 10 trading days — **measured from IBKR regular-session daily bars (`get_price_history`, `step='ONE_DAY'`, `outside_rth=false`), never from `get_price_snapshot`; Operating_Protocols.md §19 PRICE BASIS. This is the screen that has repeatedly caught D1's own snapshot-based magnitudes a week late (COIN, GLW, UPS) — measure it right here so the correction is not the mechanism.** OR — when the gap since W2's own last successful completion exceeds 10 trading days (a missed weekly cycle; owner directive 2026-07-25 CATCH-UP EVIDENCE WINDOW, see Observability § above) — on any day since that last completion, whichever span is longer (the SCREEN's look-back only; Strategy B's frozen 10-trading-day post-event ENTRY window, `strategy/04_strategy_b.md`, is unchanged and never widened by this), where the move was attributable to a public event (plus up to 3 sub-net items judged extraordinary, §19's escape valve). Event types: earnings, FDA decisions, guidance updates, regulatory actions, material corporate developments (M&A, management), analyst actions with material price impact. Layer-2 (the decider): JUDGE each surfaced name's significance in its own volatility regime and event context per §19 — the judgment, not the move percentage, decides what advances to PART 2. Per entry: ticker, name, event date, event type, move magnitude and direction, source, significance verdict (conviction 30/45/60/75 + one-sentence reason), `legacy_rule_pass` (move ≥5%, the old fixed bar — computed mechanically, record-only benchmark per §19), and `below_spec_floor` where the move is <5% (significant-but-below B's frozen Entry criterion 1 — context/SL1 evidence only, NEVER advanced as a B candidate, §19's spec-floor rail). Sort by event date (most recent first). Log ONE `entry_type='research-screen'` decision_log row (screen='post-event') per §19's logging contract — including quiet weeks (`surfaced_count=0`, empty arrays).

PART 2 — Ranked shortlist. The downstream W4 routine reads this PART 2 verbatim and enqueues thesis-construction entries to the `PENDING_ANALYSIS` queue (`events.queue_events`) for the ranked shortlist (D2 runs them), so rank explicitly and surface days-remaining-in-window per candidate.

Evaluate each entry for possible over- or under-sized reaction relative to fundamental implications. Ground in: event details, fundamentals from recent filings, comparable historical reactions to similar events at similar companies (retrieved, not recalled), information vs. sentiment distinction. Default assumption: market reaction is correct.

Rank a shortlist of up to 15 candidates (cap unchanged). Only PART 1 entries that pass BOTH the §19 significance judgment AND B's frozen spec floor (≥5% event-day move, `strategy/04_strategy_b.md` Entry criterion 1) are rankable here — `below_spec_floor` entries remain PART 1 context only. Per candidate: (a) direction and magnitude of hypothesized mispricing, (b) supporting public information, (c) convergence indicators to watch, (d) days remaining in the 10-trading-day entry window, (e) priority tier (top-5 / rest).

Exclude any name with an open Strategy A position.

OUTPUT: write the complete content (PART 1 + PART 2) directly to `Weekly_Post_Event_Screen.md`. First line is the YYYY-WW marker. Chat output: one-line acknowledgment that the file was written.
